========================================
tests/valid/arithmetic_chains.liz
========================================

-~> Longer arithmetic gives the same numbers, whatever the types.
function mix(a, b, c) {
  [a * b + c, a - b * c / 2, (a + b) * (a - b), a * 2 < b + c, a / b / c]
}
puts(mix(1, 2, 3))
puts(mix(1.5, 2, 0.25))
puts(mix(7, 2.0, 4))

let total = 0
let x = 0.5
for (let i = 0; i < 10; i += 1) {
  total += x * 1.5 - x / 4.0
  x = x + 0.25
}
puts(total, x)

let p = {"x": 3, "y": 4, "next": {"z": 0.5}}
puts(p.x * p.x + p.y * p.y, p.x * 2 + p.next.z, math.pi * 2 - math.tau)

-~> Points and strings go through the same operators.
let v = (1, 2)
puts(v + v * 2 - (0.5, 0.5), "ab" + "c" * 2 + "d")

tests/valid/arrays.liz

let a = [1, "two", true, [4], {"five": 5}]
puts(a, len(a))
puts(a[0], a[1], a[3][0], a[4]["five"], a[5], a[-1])
a[0] = 100
puts(a[0])
let b = a
b[1] = "shared"
puts(a[1])
puts([1, 2] + [3], [] + [], [1, [2, [3]]])
let nums = range(1, 6)
puts(nums, first(nums), last(nums), rest(nums), first([]), rest([]))
push(nums, 6)
puts(pop(nums), nums)

========================================
tests/valid/closures.liz
========================================

function counter() {
  let n = 0
  function() { n = n + 1 }
}
let a = counter()
let b = counter()
a()
a()
puts(a(), b())

let fs = []
for (let i = 0; i < 3; i = i + 1) {
  let j = i
  push(fs, function() { j * 10 })
}
puts(fs[0](), fs[1](), fs[2]())

let late = function() { definedLater }
let definedLater = "seen"
puts(late())

========================================
tests/valid/colors.liz
========================================

-~> Colors: hex literals, channels with dot access, and the builders.
let green = #3fa34d
puts(green, type(green))
puts(green.r, green.g, green.b, green.a)

-~> #rgb doubles each digit, any case works, and alpha prints only if not 255.
puts(#f00, #FFAA00, #3fa34d80, #00000000)
puts(#3fa34d == #3FA34D, #fff === #ffffff, #fff == #ffffff00, #fff != #000)

-~> The builders: floats are rounded.
puts(rgb(255, 128, 0), rgba(10, 20, 30, 128), rgb(0.4, 254.6, 127.5))
puts(hsl(0, 100, 50), hsl(120, 100, 50), hsl(240, 100, 50), hsl(210, 50, 40))
puts(hsl(0, 0, 100), hsl(360, 100, 50), hsl(60, 100, 25))

-~> Colors are values: truthy, comparable, and fine in arrays.
if (#000) { puts("truthy") }
let palette = [#f00, #0f0, #00f]
puts(palette, len(palette), palette[1].g)
puts(str(#abc), #abc == "#aabbcc")

-~> A color can't be changed: make a new one.
let fade = function(c, alpha) { return rgba(c.r, c.g, c.b, alpha) }
puts(fade(green, 100))

let a = 5; let b = 6 -~> a comment after code
let c = 7
;;
puts(a + b + c);
puts(10 // 3) -~> // is integer division, not a comment
let long = 1 +
  2 +
  3
puts(long)

========================================
tests/valid/conditionals.liz

function grade(n) {
  if (n >= 90) {
    "A"
  } elif (n >= 80) {
    "B"
  } elif (n >= 70) {
    if (n >= 75) { "C+" } else { "C" }
  } else {
    "F"
  }
}
puts(grade(95), grade(85), grade(77), grade(71), grade(10))
let x = if (false) { 1 }
puts(x)
if (0) { puts("no") } elif ("") { puts("no") } elif ([1]) { puts("[1] is true") }

========================================
tests/valid/continue_and_compound.liz
========================================

-~> continue skips to the next round.
let odds = []
for (let i = 0; i < 10; i += 1) {
  if (i % 2 == 0) { continue }
  push(odds, i)
}
puts(odds)

-~> In a for loop the update still runs; in a while loop you update yourself.
let n = 0
let total = 0
while (n < 6) {
  n += 1
  if (n == 3) { continue }
  total += n
}
puts(total)

-~> Only the innermost loop is skipped.
let pairs = 0
for (let a = 0; a < 3; a += 1) {
  for (let b = 0; b < 3; b += 1) {
    if (a == b) { continue }
    pairs += 1
  }
}
puts(pairs)

-~> Compound assignment.
let x = 10
x += 5
x -= 3
x *= 2
x /= 4
puts(x)
let s = "ab"
s += "cd"
s *= 2
puts(s)

let list = [1, 2, 3]
list[1] += 40
list[0] *= 7
puts(list)

let thing = {"count": 1, "inner": {"n": 2}}
thing.count += 1
thing.inner.n *= 5
puts(thing)

-~> The target is read and written once.
let calls = 0
function index() { calls += 1; 0 }
let box = [100]
box[index()] += 1
puts(box, calls)

-~> math.asin and math.acos.
puts(math.acos(1), math.asin(0))
puts(math.abs(math.acos(0.5) - 60deg) < 0.000001)
puts(math.abs(math.asin(1) - 90deg) < 0.000001)

========================================
tests/valid/default_parameters.liz
========================================

-~> Default values for parameters.
function t(key, values = null) {
  if (values == null) { return key }
  key + ":" + str(values)
}
puts(t("hello"))
puts(t("hello", 3))

-~> A default can use the parameters before it.
function box(w, h = w * 2, label = "box " + str(w) + "x" + str(h)) {
  [w, h, label]
}
puts(box(2))
puts(box(2, 5))
puts(box(2, 5, "wide"))

-~> Defaults run at each call, so a list default is a new list every time.
function collect(item, into = []) {
  push(into, item)
  into
}
puts(collect(1))
puts(collect(2))
puts(collect(3, [9]))

-~> Function literals and closures too.
let scale = 10
let grow = function(n, by = scale) { n + by }
puts(grow(1))
scale = 20
puts(grow(1))
puts(grow(1, 1))

-~> All parameters can have defaults.
function pair(a = 1, b = 2) { a * 10 + b }
puts(pair(), pair(5), pair(5, 6))

-~> A name called default is still a name.
let default = 7
puts(default)

========================================
tests/valid/equality.liz
========================================

puts(1 == 1, 1 == 2, 1 != 2)
puts(1 == true, 0 == false, 2 == true, "5" == 5, "05" == 5, "five" == 5)
puts(1 === true, "5" === 5, 5 === 5, "a" === "a", 1 !== true)
puts([1, 2] == [1, 2], [1, "2"] == [1, 2], [1, "2"] === [1, 2], [1] == [1, 1])
puts({"a": 1} == {"a": 1}, {"a": 1} == {"a": 2}, {1: 1} == {"1": 1})
let n = if (false) { 1 }
puts(n == n, n == 0, n == false)
let f = function() {}
puts(f == f, f == function() {}, len == len)

========================================
tests/valid/escapes_and_dots.liz
========================================

-~> Escapes: \" \\ \n \t
puts("say \"hi\"")
puts("back\\slash")
puts("two\nlines")

puts(len("a\nb"))
puts("\\n stays as it is")

-~> Dot access is the same as indexing with a string
let ana = {"name": "Ana", "pet": {"kind": "lizard"}, "tags": ["a", "b"]}
puts(ana.name)
puts(ana.pet.kind)
puts(ana.tags[1])
puts(ana.missing)

ana.age = 20
ana.pet.kind = "gecko"
puts(ana["age"], ana.pet["kind"])

let counter = {"n": 0, "inc": function() { 1 }}
counter.n = counter.n + counter.inc()
puts(counter.n)

function make() { {"x": 7} }
puts(make().x)

========================================
tests/valid/exponents_and_else_if.liz
========================================

-~> Exponent float literals: 1e3, 2.5e-4, 1E+6. They are always floats.
puts(1e3, 2.5e-4, 1E+6, 5e0)
puts(type(1e3), type(5e0))
puts(1e3 + 1, 2e2 / 4, 1e-3 * 1000)
puts(1e21, 1e-7)
puts(1e3 == 1000, 1e3 === 1000)
puts(1e2deg == 100deg)

-~> An 'e' with no digits after it is not an exponent.
let e = 2
puts(3 + e)
function pick(n) {
  if (n < 0) { "negative" }
  else if (n == 0) { "zero" }
  else if (n < 10) { "small" }
  elif (n < 100) { "medium" }
  else { "large" }
}

-~> "else if" is the same as "elif".
puts(pick(-5), pick(0), pick(7), pick(50), pick(500))
puts(if (false) { 1 } else if (false) { 2 })
puts(if (false) { 1 } else if (true) { 2 })

========================================
tests/valid/floats.liz
========================================

-~> Decimal numbers: literals, mixed arithmetic, comparison and printing.
puts(1.5, 10.0, 0.25)
puts(1.5 + 1, 2 * 0.5, 3 - 0.5, -1.5)

-~> Integers with integers are untouched.
puts(1 / 2, 7 // 2, -7 % 3)
-~> With a float on either side, / is real division.
puts(1 / 2.0, 1.0 / 4, 1 / 3.0)
puts(7.5 // 2, -7.5 // 2, 7.5 % 2, -7.5 % 2, 7.5 % -2)
let a = -7.5
let b = 2.0
puts((a // b) * b + a % b == a)

puts(2.0 ** 3, 2 ** 3.0, 2.0 ** -1, 4 ** 0.5, 2 ** 10)

-~> Printing always shows a '.' or an exponent.
puts(str(1.0), str(100.0), str(0.1), 0.1 + 0.2)
puts(10.0 ** 21, 10.0 ** 20, 0.000001, 0.0000001)

-~> Comparison works across types by value.
puts(1.5 < 2, 2 < 1.5, 1.0 <= 1, 2.5 >= 2.5)
puts(1 == 1.0, 1 === 1.0, "1.5" == 1.5, "1.5" === 1.5, 0.0 == -0.0)
puts([1, 2.0] == [1.0, 2], [1, 2.0] === [1.0, 2])

-~> Truthiness and types.
puts(if (0.0) { "true" } else { "false" }, !0.0, !0.5)
puts(type(1.5), type(1), type(1 + 0.5), type(2 // 1.0))

-~> Conversions.
puts(int(1.9), int(-1.9), int(0.5), int(3.0))
puts(float(3), float(1.5), float("2.25"), float("7"))
puts(type(float(1)), type(int(float(1))))

-~> A float inside arrays and hashes.
puts([1.5, 2.0], {"x": 0.5})
let point = {"x": 1.5, "y": 2.5}
puts(point.x + point.y)

========================================
tests/valid/functions_recursion.liz
========================================

function add(a, b) { return a + b }
function noReturn() { }
function early(x) {
  if (x > 0) { return "positive" }
  return "not positive"
}
puts(add(2, 3), noReturn(), early(5), early(-5))

function factorial(n) { if (n <= 1) { 1 } else { n * factorial(n - 1) } }
function fib(n) { if (n < 2) { n } else { fib(n - 1) + fib(n - 2) } }
function sumTo(n) { if (n == 0) { 0 } else { n + sumTo(n - 1) } }
puts(factorial(20), fib(25), sumTo(5000))

function ackermann(m, n) {
  if (m == 0) { return n + 1 }
  if (n == 0) { return ackermann(m - 1, 1) }
  ackermann(m - 1, ackermann(m, n - 1))
}
puts(ackermann(2, 3))
puts(function(x) { x * 2 }(21))

========================================
tests/valid/hash_library.liz
========================================

-~> The hash library, list.copy and same.
let shape = {"kind": "circle", "x": 10, "y": 20}
let moved = hash.copy(shape)
moved.x = 15
puts(shape, moved)
puts(hash.keys(shape), hash.values(shape))

let style = {"fill": #ff0000, "kind": "disc"}
let merged = hash.merge(shape, style)
puts(merged)
puts(shape)

puts(hash.has(shape, "x"), hash.has(shape, "fill"), hash.has({"empty": null}, "empty"))
puts(hash.remove(moved, "y"), hash.remove(moved, "y"), moved)

-~> same asks for the same container, not equal contents.
let a = [1, 2]
let b = a
let c = list.copy(a)
puts(same(a, b), same(a, c), a == c)
puts(same(shape, hash.copy(shape)), shape == hash.copy(shape))
puts(same(1, 1), same(1, 1.0), same("hi", "h" + "i"), same((1, 2), (1, 2)))

-~> A cache keyed by identity.
let seen = []
function visit(h) {
  for (let i = 0; i < len(seen); i += 1) {
    if (same(seen[i], h)) { return "again" }
  }
  push(seen, h)
  "new"
}
puts(visit(shape), visit(moved), visit(shape), visit(hash.copy(shape)))

========================================
tests/valid/layers.liz
========================================

-~> A layer is a named block, and its body is whatever is indented more.
let total = 0
let log = []

layer sun
  let size = 10
  total = total + size
  push(log, "in sun")
  layer rays
    let size = 3
    total = total + size
    push(log, "in rays")
  push(log, "back in sun")

layer "big moon" -~> a name with spaces needs quotes
  total = total + 100

layer empty

push(log, "after")
puts(total)
puts(log)

-~> Variables made in a layer stay in it.
let outside = 1
layer scratch
  let outside = 2
  puts(outside)
puts(outside)

-~> Layers work inside braces, measured from the layer's own column.
function paint(n) {
  let drawn = 0
  for (let i = 0; i < n; i = i + 1) {
    layer stroke
      drawn = drawn + 1
      if (drawn == 2) { break }
  }
  layer result
    return drawn
  return -1
}
puts(paint(5))

-~> return inside a layer ends the function, a loop break ends the loop.
function find(list, x) {
  for (let i = 0; i < len(list); i = i + 1) {
    layer check
      if (list[i] == x) { return i }
  }
  return -1
}
puts(find([4, 5, 6], 6), find([4, 5, 6], 9))

-~> layer is still an ordinary name everywhere else.
let layer = 1
layer = layer + 1
puts(layer, layer * 2)
function twice(layer) { layer * 2 }
puts(twice(layer))

let evens = []
while (i < 10) {

}

  for (let j = 0; j < 5; j = j + 1) {
    if (j > k) { break }
    total = total + 1
  }
}
puts(total)

let n = 0
for (;;) {
  n = n + 1
  if (n == 4) { break }
}
puts(n)
puts(while (false) {})

========================================
tests/valid/math_and_units.liz
========================================

-~> The math library, reached with dot access, and angle units.
puts(math.pi, math.tau, math.tau == 2 * math.pi)
puts(math.sin(0), math.cos(0), math.tan(0))
puts(math.sin(90deg), math.cos(180deg))
puts(math.atan2(1, 1) == 45deg, math.atan2(0, -1) == math.pi)
puts(math.sqrt(16), math.sqrt(2.25), math.abs(-3), math.abs(-1.5))
puts(math.floor(1.9), math.floor(-1.1), math.ceil(1.1), math.ceil(-1.9))
puts(math.round(1.5), math.round(2.5), math.round(-1.5), math.round(-2.5), math.round(1.4))
puts(math.min(3, 1, 2), math.max(3, 1, 2), math.min(1.5, 2), math.max(1, 2.5))
puts(type(math.round(2.5)), type(math.abs(-3)), type(math.sqrt(4)))

-~> random is deterministic: the same seed gives the same numbers.
math.seed(7)
let first = [math.random(), math.random(1, 100), math.random(1, 100)]
math.seed(7)
let second = [math.random(), math.random(1, 100), math.random(1, 100)]
puts(first == second, first === second)
let r = math.random()
puts(r >= 0 && r < 1)
let n = math.random(5, 5)
puts(n)

-~> Units: deg, rad and turn give radians as a float; px keeps the number.
puts(180deg, 45deg, 1.5rad, 1rad, 0.25turn, 1turn)
puts(10px, 10.5px, type(10px), type(10.5px), type(45deg))
puts(90deg == 0.25turn, 360deg == 1turn, 180deg == math.pi)
puts(math.sin(90deg), math.cos(0.5turn))

-~> A variable called math hides the library.
function hide() {
  let math = {"pi": 3}
  math.pi
}
puts(hide(), math.pi)

-~> A number followed by a dot and a name is still dot access.
let h = {"x": 5}
puts(h.x * 2.5)

========================================
tests/valid/modules.liz
========================================

-~> use: every name of a module.
use "modules/geometry"
puts(square(3), sides)

-~> The names are alive: a function that changes the module's variable.
puts(made())
square(1)
puts(made())

-~> A private name (it starts with _) isn't seen.
try { _made } catch (e) { puts(e.message) }

-~> = changes the module's own variable; a let hides it instead.
use "modules/counter"
bump()
count = 10
puts(bump(), count)

-~> A module runs once per program, however many files use it. The hash
-~> of use ... as is made when the module has run: its count is still 0,
-~> but its functions work on the module's own variables.
use "modules/counter" as counter
puts(counter.count, keys(counter))
puts(counter.bump(), count)

-~> A module that uses another one: its own names only.
use "modules/shapes/all" as shapes
puts(shapes.cube(2), keys(shapes))

========================================
tests/valid/null_value.liz
========================================

-~> null can be written in code.
let nothing = null
puts(nothing)
puts(type(null))

========================================
tests/valid/points.liz
========================================

-~> Points: (x, y) or (x, y, z), arithmetic per coordinate, dot access.
let p = (1, 2)
let q = (3, 4)
puts(p, q, type(p), p.x, p.y)
puts(p + q, q - p, p * 3, 3 * p, q / 2, -p)

-~> Coordinates keep their type, and the numeric rules are the ones of numbers.
puts((0.5, 2), (1, 2) + (0.5, 1), (3, 4) / 2, (3, 4) / 2.0)
puts((1, 2) * 0.5, 2 * (1.5, 2), (1, 2) == (1.0, 2), (1, 2) === (1.0, 2), (1, 2) === (1, 2))
puts((1, 2) != (1, 3), (1, 2) == (1, 2, 3), (1, 2) == [1, 2])

-~> Three coordinates, and a call with one point as the argument.
let c = (1, 2, 3)
puts(c, c.z, c + (1, 1, 1), c * 2, -c, (1, 2, 3) == (1, 2, 3))
let shift = function(pt) { return pt + (10, 10) }
puts(shift((1, 2)), shift(point(5, 5)))

-~> The point builder and math.dist, math.angle.
puts(point(1, 2), point(1.5, 2, 3), math.dist((0, 0), (3, 4)), math.dist((1, 2, 3), (1, 2, 5)))
puts(math.angle((0, 0), (1, 1)) == 45deg, math.angle((0, 0), (0, -1)) == -90deg)

-~> Points are values: truthy, in arrays, and in expressions.
let corners = [(0, 0), (10, 0), (10, 10)]
puts(corners, corners[2].x + corners[2].y)
let mid = (corners[0] + corners[2]) / 2
puts(mid, (1 + 2, 3 * 4), ((1, 2)))

tests/valid/scopes.liz
========================================

let x = "global"
function f() {
  let x = "f"
  function g() {
    let x = "g"
    x
  }
  [x, g()]
}
puts(f(), x)

let count = 0
function bump() { count = count + 1 }
bump()
bump()
puts(count)

function shadow(count) {
  count = count + 100
  count
}
puts(shadow(1), count)

if (true) { let inner = 1; x = "changed by the if" }
puts(x)

for (let x = 0; x < 1; x = x + 1) { puts("loop x", x) }
puts(x)

let len = function(v) { "my own len" }
puts(len([1, 2]))

========================================
tests/valid/statements_end_at_newline.liz
========================================

-~> A statement ends at the end of its line.
let x = 10
-1
puts(x)

-~> It goes on when the line ends with an operator, a comma or an
-~> opening bracket.
let total = 1 +
  2 *
  3
puts(total)
let ok = total > 5 &&
  x == 10
puts(ok)
let list = [
  1,
  2
]
puts(list)

-~> Inside an open ( or [ or a hash, a new line is just a space.
puts((1
  - 1))
let both = (x > 5
  && total > 5)
puts(both)
let h = {
  "sum": 1
    + 2,
  "list": [3
    - 1],
}
puts(h["sum"], h["list"])

-~> A function body inside brackets is a block again: lines end there.
let results = [function() {
  let y = 1
  -2
  y
}]
puts(results[0]())

-~> ; still separates statements on one line.
let a = 1; let b = a - 1
puts(a, b)

-~> A return at the end of its line returns nothing.
function nothing() {
  return
  5
}
puts(nothing())

========================================
tests/valid/try_catch.liz
========================================

-~> try / catch.
try {
  puts("start")
  let r = 1 + true
  puts("never printed")
} catch (e) {
  puts("caught:", e.message)
  puts(e.line, e.column)
}

-~> catch without a name, and the value of the statement.
let v = 0
try { nope } catch { v = 1 }
puts(v)
function safe() {
  try { error("boom") } catch (e) { "recovered from " + e.message }
}
puts(safe())

-~> Errors from deep inside functions and from builtins.
function deep(n) {
  if (n == 0) { return math.sqrt(-1) }
  deep(n - 1)
}
try { deep(5) } catch (e) { puts(e.message) }
try { len(5) } catch (e) { puts(e.message) }
try { [1, 2][0] / 0 } catch (e) { puts(e.message) }

-~> Nothing fails: the catch is skipped.
try { puts("fine") } catch (e) { puts("not here") }

-~> The name belongs to the catch block only.
let e = "mine"
try { nope } catch (e) { puts(type(e)) }
puts(e)

-~> return, break and continue go through.
function first(items) {
  for (let i = 0; i < len(items); i += 1) {
    try {
      if (items[i] > 2) { return items[i] }
    } catch (e) {}
  }
  null
}
puts(first([1, 2, 3, 4]))

let rounds = 0
while (true) {
  rounds += 1
  try { if (rounds == 3) { break } } catch (e) {}
}
puts(rounds)

-~> A catch can fail too; an outer try catches that.
try {
  try { nope } catch (inner) { error("again: " + inner.message) }
} catch (outer) {
  puts(outer.message)
}

-~> try and catch are ordinary names elsewhere.
let try = 2
let catch = 3
puts(try + catch)
if try { puts("try is true") }

-~> Variables: declare with let, read by name, change with =.

let name = "Liz"
let toes = 20

toes = toes - 1

let a = 0
let b = 0
a = b = 7
puts("a =", a, "and b =", b)

-~> let again in the same scope replaces the variable.
let toes = "many"
puts("toes is now", toes)

========================================
examples/02_expressions.liz
========================================

-~> Arithmetic, comparison and logical expressions, with precedence.

puts("1 + 2 * 3 =", 1 + 2 * 3)
puts("(1 + 2) * 3 =", (1 + 2) * 3)
puts("10 - 4 - 3 =", 10 - 4 - 3, "(left associative)")
puts("2 ** 3 ** 2 =", 2 ** 3 ** 2, "(right associative)")
puts("-2 ** 2 =", -2 ** 2, "(** is stronger than -)")

-~> / rounds towards zero, // rounds down, % goes with //.
puts("7 / 2 =", 7 / 2, " -7 / 2 =", -7 / 2)
puts("7 // 2 =", 7 // 2, " -7 // 2 =", -7 // 2)
puts("7 % 3 =", 7 % 3, " -7 % 3 =", -7 % 3)

puts("3 < 5 is", 3 < 5, " 3 >= 5 is", 3 >= 5)
puts("abc < abd as strings is", "abc" < "abd")

-~> && binds tighter than ||.
puts("true || false && false is", true || false && false)
puts("!(1 > 2) is", !(1 > 2))

-~> == converts between types, === doesn't.
puts("5 == the string 5 is", 5 == "5", " 5 === the string 5 is", 5 === "5")
puts("1 == true is", 1 == true, " 1 === true is", 1 === true)

-~> Strings: + joins them, * repeats them.
puts("Liz" + "ard", "ab" * 3)

----------------------------------------

========================================
examples/03_conditionals.liz
========================================

-~> if, elif and else, nested conditionals, and if as a value.

function describe(temperature) {
  if (temperature < 15) {
    puts(temperature, "-> too cold, the lizard hides")
  } elif (temperature < 30) {
    if (temperature >= 25) {
      puts(temperature, "-> perfect, the lizard sunbathes")
    } else {
      puts(temperature, "-> fine, the lizard walks around")
    }
  } else {
    puts(temperature, "-> too hot, the lizard looks for shade")
  }
}

describe(10)
describe(20)
describe(27)
describe(35)

-~> An if gives the value of the block that ran.
let toes = 4
let label = if (toes == 5) { "complete" } else { "missing toes" }
puts("the foot is", label)

-~> Conditions accept any value: 0, "", [], {} and null count as false.
if (0) { puts("not shown") } else { puts("0 counts as false") }
if ("Liz") { puts("a non-empty string counts as true") }
if ([]) { puts("not shown") } else { puts("an empty array counts as false") }

========================================
examples/04_loops.liz
========================================

-~> while and for loops, break, and nested loops.

let countdown = 3
while (countdown > 0) {
  puts("countdown:", countdown)
  countdown = countdown - 1
}

let total = 0
for (let i = 1; i <= 100; i = i + 1) {
  total = total + i
}
puts("1 + 2 + ... + 100 =", total)

-~> break leaves the innermost loop.
let n = 1
while (true) {
  if (n * n > 50) {
    break
  }
  n = n + 1
}
puts("the first n with n * n > 50 is", n)

-~> A multiplication table with two loops.
for (let row = 1; row <= 3; row = row + 1) {
  let line = ""
  for (let col = 1; col <= 3; col = col + 1) {
    line = line + str(row * col) + " "
  }
  puts(line)
}

========================================
examples/05_functions.liz
========================================

-~> Functions: definition, parameters, return values and recursion.

function add(a, b) {
  return a + b
}
puts("add(2, 3) =", add(2, 3))

-~> Without return, a function gives the value of its last statement.
function square(x) {
  x * x
}
puts("square(9) =", square(9))

-~> A function can also be stored in a variable.
let greet = function(name) { "Hello, " + name + "!" }
puts(greet("Liz"))

-~> Recursion.
function factorial(n) {
  if (n <= 1) {
    return 1
  }
  return n * factorial(n - 1)
}
puts("factorial(10) =", factorial(10))

function fib(n) {
  if (n < 2) {
    return n
  }
  fib(n - 1) + fib(n - 2)
}
puts("fib(20) =", fib(20))

-~> Mutual recursion: each function calls the other.
function isEven(n) { if (n == 0) { true } else { isOdd(n - 1) } }
function isOdd(n) { if (n == 0) { false } else { isEven(n - 1) } }
puts("isEven(10) =", isEven(10), " isOdd(7) =", isOdd(7))

----------------------------------------

========================================
examples/06_scopes.liz
========================================

-~> Global and local variables, and the same name in different scopes.

let x = "global"

function show() {
  let x = "local to show"
  puts("inside show, x is", x)
}
show()
puts("after show, x is still", x)

-~> A block has its own scope: let inside it creates a new variable.
if (true) {
  let x = "local to the if"
  puts("inside the if, x is", x)
}
puts("after the if, x is", x)

-~> Without let, = changes the variable of the outer scope.
let counter = 0
function increment() {
  counter = counter + 1
}
increment()
increment()
puts("counter after two increments:", counter)

-~> Parameters are local: they hide the global with the same name.
let value = 100
function double(value) {
  value * 2
}
puts("double(5) =", double(5), "and value is still", value)

-~> The variable of a for header only exists inside the loop.
let i = "outside"
for (let i = 0; i < 2; i = i + 1) {
  puts("in the loop, i is", i)
}
puts("after the loop, i is", i)

========================================
examples/07_data_structures.liz
========================================

-~> Arrays and hashes.

let toes = [5, 5, 4, 5]
puts("toes:", toes, "length:", len(toes))
puts("first foot:", toes[0], "third foot:", toes[2])
toes[2] = 5
puts("after fixing the third foot:", toes)
push(toes, 6)
puts("after push:", toes)
puts("pop gives", pop(toes), "and leaves", toes)
puts("first:", first(toes), "last:", last(toes), "rest:", rest(toes))
puts("joined:", [1, 2] + [3])
puts("out of range gives", toes[10])

let lizard = {"name": "Liz", "age": 3, "colors": ["green", "brown"]}
puts("lizard:", lizard)
puts("name:", lizard["name"], "second color:", lizard["colors"][1])
lizard["age"] = lizard["age"] + 1
lizard["tail"] = true
puts("keys:", keys(lizard))
puts("values:", values(lizard))
delete(lizard, "tail")
puts("without the tail:", lizard)
puts("a missing key gives", lizard["wings"])

-~> Arrays and hashes are shared: a function can change them.
let lizards = [
  {"name": "Liz", "age": 3},
  {"name": "Iggy", "age": 7}
]
function birthday(l) {
  l["age"] = l["age"] + 1
}
for (let i = 0; i < len(lizards); i = i + 1) {
  birthday(lizards[i])
}
puts("after the birthdays:", lizards)

========================================
examples/08_builtins.liz
========================================

-~> The builtin functions, including type conversion.

puts("puts writes its arguments separated by spaces:", 1, true, [2])

puts("len of a string:", len("lizard"), " of an array:", len([1, 2]), " of a hash:", len({"a": 1}))

puts("type(5) =", type(5), " type of a string:", type("5"), " type([]) =", type([]))
puts("type(len) =", type(len), " type of a function:", type(function() {}))

-~> Conversion between types.
let text = "41"
let number = int(text) + 1
puts("int of the string 41, plus 1:", number)
puts("str(42) + an exclamation mark:", str(42) + "!")
puts("int(true) =", int(true), " int(false) =", int(false))

-~> range builds arrays of numbers.
puts("range(5) =", range(5), " range(2, 6) =", range(2, 6))

-~> Array and hash helpers.
let a = [1, 2, 3]
push(a, 4)
puts("after push:", a)
puts("pop:", pop(a), " first:", first(a), " last:", last(a), " rest:", rest(a))
let h = {"b": 2, "a": 1}
puts("keys:", keys(h), " values:", values(h))
puts("delete:", delete(h, "b"), " and the hash is now", h)

========================================
examples/09_extensions.liz
========================================

-~> Optional extensions: closures, short-circuit evaluation and
-~> higher-order functions.

-~> Closures: the returned function remembers count.
function makeCounter() {
  let count = 0
  return function() {
    count = count + 1
    count
  }
}
let counterA = makeCounter()
let counterB = makeCounter()
counterA()
counterA()
puts("counterA:", counterA(), " counterB:", counterB())

function adder(x) {
  function(y) { x + y }
}
let addTen = adder(10)
puts("addTen(5) =", addTen(5))

-~> Short-circuit: the right side only runs when it is needed.
let calls = 0
function noisy() {
  calls = calls + 1
  true
}
false && noisy()
true || noisy()
puts("after false && noisy() and true || noisy(), noisy ran", calls, "times")

-~> So this doesn't divide by zero:
let d = 0
puts("d != 0 && 10 / d > 1 is", d != 0 && 10 / d > 1)

-~> Higher-order functions: functions that take or return functions.
function map(list, f) {
  let result = []
  for (let i = 0; i < len(list); i = i + 1) {
    push(result, f(list[i]))
  }
  result
}

function filter(list, keep) {
  let result = []
  for (let i = 0; i < len(list); i = i + 1) {
    if (keep(list[i])) {
      push(result, list[i])
    }
  }
  result
}

function reduce(list, f, start) {
  let acc = start
  for (let i = 0; i < len(list); i = i + 1) {
    acc = f(acc, list[i])
  }
  acc
}

let evens = filter(range(1, 11), function(n) { n % 2 == 0 })
puts("evens:", evens)
puts("their squares:", map(evens, function(n) { n ** 2 }))
puts("their sum:", reduce(evens, function(a, b) { a + b }, 0))

function compose(f, g) {
  function(x) { f(g(x)) }
}
let squareThenAddTen = compose(addTen, function(x) { x * x })
puts("squareThenAddTen(3) =", squareThenAddTen(3))

----------------------------------------

========================================
examples/10_floats_and_math.liz
========================================

-~> Decimal numbers, the math library and angle units: enough to draw
-~> a circle and a wave.

-~> Floats: a float on either side makes real division.
puts(7 / 2, 7 / 2.0, 7 // 2.0, 7.5 % 2)
puts(0.1 + 0.2, 1 == 1.0, 1 === 1.0, int(2.9), float(3))

-~> Angles are radians. deg, rad and turn are units, px keeps the number.
puts(180deg == math.pi, 0.25turn, 10px)

-~> Points on a circle of radius 100px, every 90 degrees.
let radius = 100px
for (let i = 0; i < 4; i = i + 1) {
  let angle = i * 90deg
  let x = math.round(radius * math.cos(angle))
  let y = math.round(radius * math.sin(angle))
  puts("angle", i * 90, "-> (" + str(x) + ", " + str(y) + ")")
}

-~> A wave, sampled at six points, as bars of '#'.
for (let i = 0; i < 6; i = i + 1) {
  let height = math.round(5 + 4 * math.sin(i * 1turn / 6))
  puts(i, "#" * height)
}

-~> random always gives the same numbers unless it is seeded differently.
math.seed(2026)
let dice = [math.random(1, 6), math.random(1, 6), math.random(1, 6)]
math.seed(2026)
puts(dice == [math.random(1, 6), math.random(1, 6), math.random(1, 6)])

-~> sqrt, atan2, min and max.
puts(math.sqrt(2), math.atan2(1, 1) / 1deg, math.min(3, 1.5), math.max(3, 1.5))

========================================
examples/11_colors_and_points.liz
========================================

-~> Colors and points: the two values a drawing is made of.

-~> A color is #rgb, #rrggbb or #rrggbbaa. Its channels are read with dot access.
let leaf = #3fa34d
puts(leaf, leaf.r, leaf.g, leaf.b, leaf.a)
puts(rgb(255, 128, 0), rgba(0, 0, 0, 128), hsl(210, 50, 40))

-~> A color can't be changed, so a variation is a new color.
let see_through = function(c, alpha) { return rgba(c.r, c.g, c.b, alpha) }
puts(see_through(leaf, 100))

-~> A point is (x, y), or (x, y, z). Arithmetic works on each coordinate.
let a = (10, 20)
let b = (40, 60)
puts(a + b, b - a, a * 2, b / 2, -a)
puts("x is", a.x, "and y is", a.y)

-~> Distances and angles between points.
puts(math.dist(a, b), math.angle(a, b))

-~> Points on a circle around a center.
let center = (100, 100)
for (let i = 0; i < 4; i = i + 1) {
  let angle = i * 90deg
  let spot = center + (math.round(50 * math.cos(angle)), math.round(50 * math.sin(angle)))
  puts(spot)
}

========================================
examples/12_layers.liz
========================================

-~> Layers, and the text and list helpers.

-~> A layer is a named group of statements. Its body is every line indented
-~> more than the word layer, so there are no braces.
let picture = []

layer sky
  push(picture, "sun")
  layer clouds
    push(picture, "cloud")
  push(picture, "birds")

layer "tall grass"
  push(picture, "grass")

puts(picture)

-~> Variables made in a layer stay in it, but outer ones can be changed.
let count = 0
layer counting
  let step = 5
  count = count + step
puts(count)

-~> text cuts and joins strings. Indexes count characters.
let title = "  Lizard Script  "
let clean = text.trim(title)
puts(clean, text.slice(clean, 0, 6), text.find(clean, "Script"))
puts(text.split("red,green,blue", ","))
puts(text.join(["a", "b", "c"], "+"), text.upper(clean))
puts(text.number("2.5") * 2, text.number("two"))

-~> list reorders arrays. sort and reverse make new arrays.
let scores = [30, 10, 20]
puts(list.sort(scores), list.reverse(scores), scores)
puts(list.sort(scores, function(a, b) { a > b }))
list.insert(scores, 1, 99)
list.remove(scores, 0)
puts(scores, list.index(scores, 20))

========================================
examples/13_defaults_try_continue.liz
========================================

-~> Default parameters, try / catch, continue and compound assignment.

-~> A parameter can have a default value. It is worked out at each call,
-~> after the parameters before it, so it can use them.
function label(name, size = 12, caption = name + " (" + str(size) + ")") {
  caption
}
puts(label("tree"))
puts(label("tree", 20))
puts(label("tree", 20, "big tree"))

-~> Parameters with a default come last. A missing one without a default is
-~> still an error.

-~> try / catch turns a runtime error into a value you can look at.
function divide(a, b) {
  if (b == 0) { error("can't divide " + str(a) + " by zero") }
  a / b
}
try {
  puts(divide(10, 2))
  puts(divide(1, 0))
  puts("not reached")
} catch (e) {
  puts("caught:", e.message)
}

-~> The catch gets a hash with message, line and column. The name is optional.
try { nope } catch (err) { puts(err.line, err.column, err.message) }
try { nope } catch { puts("something failed") }

-~> A try is a statement, with the value of the block that ran. As the last
-~> statement of a function, that is what the function gives.
function to_number(text, fallback = 0) {
  try { int(text) } catch { fallback }
}
puts(to_number("12"), to_number("twelve"), to_number("twelve", -1))

-~> continue skips the rest of a round. In a for loop the update still runs.
let kept = []
for (let i = 1; i <= 10; i += 1) {
  if (i % 3 == 0) { continue }
  push(kept, i)
}
puts(kept)

-~> Compound assignment: += -= *= /= on names, list items and hash keys.
let totals = {"sum": 0, "count": 0}
let values = [4, 8, 15, 16, 23, 42]
for (let i = 0; i < len(values); i += 1) {
  try {
    if (values[i] == 15) { error("skip 15") }
    totals.sum += values[i]
    totals.count += 1
  } catch (e) {
    puts("skipped:", e.message)
    continue
  }
}
puts(totals, totals.sum / totals.count)
values[0] *= 10
puts(values)

-~> math.asin and math.acos are the inverses of sin and cos.
puts(math.round(math.acos(0.5) / 1deg), math.round(math.asin(1) / 1deg))

----------------------------------------

========================================
examples/fizzbuzz.liz
========================================

-~> Prints the numbers from 1 to 15, but multiples of 3 are "Fizz",
-~> multiples of 5 are "Buzz" and multiples of both are "FizzBuzz".

for (let i = 1; i <= 15; i = i + 1) {
  if (i % 15 == 0) {
    puts("FizzBuzz")
  } elif (i % 3 == 0) {
    puts("Fizz")
  } elif (i % 5 == 0) {
    puts("Buzz")
  } else {
    puts(i)
  }
}

========================================
examples/terrarium/clock.liz
========================================

-~> An analog clock drawn with spans: the face is a disc, the hands are
-~> thick lines. Esc quits.
-~> Run it with: lizardscript examples/terrarium/clock.liz

let size = 320
let center = (size / 2, size / 2)
let w = window.open(size, size, "clock", {"scale": 1, "fps": 30})

-~> A filled circle, one span per row. (/ of two integers is a whole
-~> number, so the fractions below divide by a float.)
function disc(c, r, color) {
  let rows = math.floor(r)
  for (let dy = -rows; dy <= rows; dy += 1) {
    let half = math.sqrt(r * r - dy * dy)
    window.span(w, c.y + dy, c.x - half, c.x + half, color)
  }
}

-~> A thick line from a to b: the spans of every row it crosses, cut from
-~> the band of points closer than half its width to the segment.
function line(a, b, width, color) {
  let r = width / 2.0
  let top = math.floor(math.min(a.y, b.y) - r)
  let bottom = math.ceil(math.max(a.y, b.y) + r)
  let d = b - a
  let length2 = d.x * d.x + d.y * d.y
  let runs = []
  for (let y = top; y <= bottom; y += 1) {
    let left = null
    let right = null
    for (let x = math.floor(math.min(a.x, b.x) - r); x <= math.ceil(math.max(a.x, b.x) + r); x += 1) {
      -~> The closest point of the segment to the middle of the pixel.
      let p = (x + 0.5, y + 0.5)
      let t = math.max(0, math.min(1, ((p.x - a.x) * d.x + (p.y - a.y) * d.y) / length2))
      if (math.dist(p, a + d * t) <= r) {
        if (left == null) {
          left = x
        }
        right = x + 1
      }
    }
    if (left != null) {
      push(runs, [y, left, right])
    }
  }
  window.spans(w, runs, color)
}

-~> The end of a hand at a fraction of a turn, clockwise from 12.
function hand_end(turn, length) {
  let angle = turn * math.tau - math.pi / 2
  center + (math.cos(angle) * length, math.sin(angle) * length)
}

function draw() {
  let now = time.now()
  window.clear(w, #1d2b33)
  disc(center, 150, #f4efe6)
  for (let mark = 0; mark < 12; mark += 1) {
    disc(hand_end(mark / 12.0, 132), 4, #2b3a55)
  }
  let minutes = now.minute + now.second / 60.0
  let hours = now.hour % 12 + minutes / 60
  line(center, hand_end(hours / 12.0, 75), 9, #2b3a55)
  line(center, hand_end(minutes / 60.0, 115), 6, #2b3a55)
  line(center, hand_end(now.second / 60.0, 125), 2, #d95763)
  disc(center, 6, #d95763)
}

let shown = -1
window.run(w, function(events) {
  for (let i = 0; i < len(events); i += 1) {
    if (events[i].type == "key" && events[i].key == "Escape") {
      return false
    }
  }
  -~> Draw only when the second changes.
  let second = time.now().second
  if (second != shown) {
    shown = second
    draw()
  }
})

========================================
examples/terrarium/files.liz
========================================

-~> Files and JSON with the Terrarium: write, read, list, and a JSON round
-~> trip. Run it with: lizardscript examples/terrarium/files.liz

let dir = fs.join(fs.home(), ".lizard-files-example")
fs.mkdir(dir)

-~> Write a text file, add to it, and read it back.
let notes = fs.join(dir, "notes.txt")
fs.write(notes, "lizards like sun\n")
fs.append(notes, "and warm rocks\n")
puts(fs.read(notes))

-~> A lizard as JSON, written with an indent of 2 and read back.
let lizard = {"name": "Liz", "toes": [5, 5, 5, 5], "color": #6abe30, "home": {"rock": true}}
let path = fs.join(dir, "liz.json")
fs.write(path, json.write(lizard, 2))
puts(fs.read(path))

let back = json.parse(fs.read(path))
puts("name:", back.name, "toes:", len(back.toes), "color:", back.color)

puts("in the folder:", fs.list(dir))

-~> A mistake is an error that try can catch.
try {
  fs.read(fs.join(dir, "missing.txt"))
} catch (e) {
  puts("caught:", e.message)
}

-~> Tidy up: files first, since remove refuses a folder with something in it.
fs.remove(notes)
fs.remove(path)
fs.remove(dir)
puts("cleaned up:", !fs.exists(dir))

========================================
examples/terrarium/image.liz
========================================

-~> Make an image, draw a gradient pixel by pixel, and save it as a PNG.
-~> Run it with: lizardscript examples/terrarium/image.liz [out.png]

let w = 256
let h = 128
let picture = img.new(w, h)

for (let y = 0; y < h; y += 1) {
  for (let x = 0; x < w; x += 1) {
    -~> Red grows to the right, blue grows down.
    img.set(picture, x, y, rgb(x, 80, y * 2))
  }
}

-~> The drawing functions every target shares: a white bar with soft ends.
img.fill(picture, [20.5, 54, 215, 20], #ffffffc0)

let out = "gradient.png"
if (len(os.args()) > 0) {
  out = os.args()[0]
}
img.save(picture, out)
puts("saved", out, img.size(img.load(out)))

========================================
examples/terrarium/layers.liz
========================================

-~> The seven blend modes, side by side: in each column three discs are
-~> drawn on a layer of their own, which is then mixed onto the gradient
-~> below with that column's mode. Point at a column to see its name in
-~> the title. Esc quits.
-~> Run it with: lizardscript examples/terrarium/layers.liz
-~> or, to save a picture of it with no window: ... layers.liz shot.png

let modes = ["normal", "multiply", "screen", "overlay", "darken", "lighten", "add"]
let cell = 80
let size = (cell * len(modes), 200)
let shot = null
if (len(os.args()) > 0) {
  shot = os.args()[0]
}
let w = window.open(size.x, size.y, "layers 🦎", {"headless": shot != null, "on_demand": true})

-~> A filled disc as a list of spans, one [y, x0, x1] per row.
function disc(c, r) {
  let rows = []
  for (let dy = -r; dy <= r; dy += 1) {
    let half = math.sqrt(r * r - dy * dy)
    push(rows, [c.y + dy, c.x - half, c.x + half])
  }
  rows
}

-~> A linear gradient from a to b: the axis is the direction divided by
-~> its squared length, so t goes from 0 at a to 1 at b.
function linear(a, b, stops) {
  let d = b - a
  let length2 = float(d.x * d.x + d.y * d.y)
  {"kind": "linear", "origin": a, "axis": (d.x / length2, d.y / length2), "stops": stops}
}

-~> The backdrop: a sunset from left to right, and below it a gray ramp
-~> in eight flat steps, so each mode shows what it does to light and dark.
function backdrop() {
  let all = [[0, 0, size.x]]
  let sky = []
  for (let y = 0; y < 140; y += 1) { push(sky, [y, 0, size.x]) }
  window.spans_gradient(w, sky, linear((0, 0), (size.x, 0), [[0, #f6c453], [0.5, #e2557b], [1, #3b4cc0]]))
  let ground = []
  for (let y = 140; y < size.y; y += 1) { push(ground, [y, 0, size.x]) }
  let ramp = linear((0, 0), (size.x, 0), [[0, #000000], [1, #ffffff]])
  ramp.steps = 8
  window.spans_gradient(w, ground, ramp)
}

-~> One column: three discs on a layer, mixed with mode.
function column(i, mode) {
  let c = (i * cell + cell / 2, 136)
  window.push_layer(w)
  window.spans(w, disc(c + (0, -22), 26), #ff3030)
  window.spans(w, disc(c + (-18, 10), 26), #30d050)
  window.spans(w, disc(c + (18, 10), 26), #3070ff)
  window.pop_layer(w, mode, 0.9)
}

function paint() {
  backdrop()
  for (let i = 0; i < len(modes); i += 1) {
    column(i, modes[i])
  }
}

paint()
window.run(w, function(events) {
  for (let i = 0; i < len(events); i += 1) {
    let e = events[i]
    if (e.type == "move") {
      let i = math.min(len(modes) - 1, math.max(0, math.floor(e.pos.x / cell)))
      window.title(w, "layers 🦎 " + modes[i])
    } elif (e.type == "key" && e.key == "Escape") {
      return false
    }
  }
  if (shot != null) {
    img.save(window.screenshot(w), shot)
    return false
  }
})

========================================
examples/terrarium/paint.liz
========================================

-~> Paint with the mouse, a trackpad or a pen: drag to paint circles whose
-~> size follows the pressure. c clears, Esc quits. While you draw, the
-~> title shows the pressure, the device's raw one and the device, to tune
-~> the pressure curve (see pen.curve in docs/TERRARIUM.md).
-~> Run it with: lizardscript examples/terrarium/paint.liz

let paper = #f4efe6
let ink = #2b3a55
let w = window.open(600, 400, "paint 🦎")
window.clear(w, paper)

-~> A filled circle, one span per row. (/ of two integers is a whole
-~> number, so the fractions below divide by a float.)
function circle(center, r, color) {
  let rows = math.floor(r)
  for (let dy = -rows; dy <= rows; dy += 1) {
    let half = math.sqrt(r * r - dy * dy)
    window.span(w, center.y + dy, center.x - half, center.x + half, color)
  }
}

-~> The size of the brush for a pressure from 0 to 1.
function brush(pressure) {
  2 + pressure * 14
}

-~> A number rounded to two decimals, as text.
function hundredths(x) {
  str(math.round(x * 100) / 100.0)
}

-~> Shows the pen's pressure in the title: "pressure 0.42 (raw 0.91) · trackpad".
function show(pen) {
  window.title(w, "pressure " + hundredths(pen.pressure) + " (raw " + hundredths(pen.raw) + ") · " + pen.source)
}

-~> A drag can move far in one frame: fill the gap with circles.
function stroke(from, to, r) {
  let steps = math.max(1, math.ceil(math.dist(from, to) / (r / 2)))
  for (let i = 1; i <= steps; i += 1) {
    circle(from + (to - from) * (i / float(steps)), r, ink)
  }
}

let last = null

window.run(w, function(events) {
  for (let i = 0; i < len(events); i += 1) {
    let e = events[i]
    if (e.type == "down" && e.button == "left") {
      circle(e.pos, brush(e.pen.pressure), ink)
      show(e.pen)
      last = e.pos
    } elif (e.type == "move" && last != null) {
      stroke(last, e.pos, brush(e.pen.pressure))
      show(e.pen)
      last = e.pos
    } elif (e.type == "up") {
      window.title(w, "paint 🦎")
      last = null
    } elif (e.type == "type" && e.text == "c") {
      window.clear(w, paper)
    } elif (e.type == "key" && e.key == "Escape") {
      return false
    }
  }
})

========================================
examples/terrarium/splash.liz
========================================

-~> A splash screen that becomes the app: a frameless, transparent window
-~> with round corners and a loading bar, which after two seconds (or a
-~> click) turns into a normal window with a title bar, centered on the
-~> screen. The app has big pixels (scale 2) around a canvas surface at
-~> the screen's full resolution: drag on it to paint, with pen pressure.
-~> Esc quits.
-~> Run it with: lizardscript examples/terrarium/splash.liz
-~> or, to save the splash and the app with no window:
-~>   lizardscript examples/terrarium/splash.liz splash.png app.png

let shots = os.args()
let headless = len(shots) == 2
let w = window.open(160, 72, "splash", {"scale": 3, "frameless": true, "transparent": true, "floating": true, "headless": headless})
let started = time.ms()
let canvas = null
let last = null

-~> A rectangle with round corners as spans: each row is shortened at its
-~> ends by how far the corner circle is from the edge there.
function rounded(r, radius) {
  let rows = []
  for (let y = 0; y < r[3]; y += 1) {
    let inset = 0
    let dy = math.max(radius - y - 0.5, y + 0.5 - (r[3] - radius), 0)
    if (dy > 0) {
      inset = radius - math.sqrt(math.max(0, radius * radius - dy * dy))
    }
    push(rows, [r[1] + y, r[0] + inset, r[0] + r[2] - inset])
  }
  rows
}

-~> The splash: a card with a gradient, a sun and a bar that fills up.
function paint_splash(progress) {
  window.clear(w, #00000000)
  let card = rounded([0, 0, 160, 72], 12)
  window.spans_gradient(w, card, {"kind": "linear", "origin": (0, 0), "axis": (0, 1 / 72.0),
    "stops": [[0, #2b3a55], [1, #10182a]]})
  window.spans_gradient(w, rounded([64, 14, 32, 32], 16), {"kind": "radial", "origin": (80, 30), "scale": 1 / 16.0,
    "stops": [[0, #ffe9a8], [0.6, #f6c453], [1, #e2557b]]})
  window.fill(w, [24, 56, 112, 4], #ffffff30)
  window.fill(w, [24, 56, 112 * progress, 4], #f6c453)
}

-~> The app's frame: a toolbar and the canvas. When the window grows, the
-~> canvas keeps what was painted and the new part gets paper.
function paint_app() {
  let size = window.size(w)
  window.clear(w, #24262d)
  window.fill(w, [0, 0, size.x, 16], #31343d)
  for (let i = 0; i < 4; i += 1) {
    window.spans(w, rounded([6 + i * 14, 3, 10, 10], 3), [#e2557b, #f6c453, #30d050, #3070ff][i])
  }
  let painted = null
  if (canvas != null) {
    painted = img.new(window.size(canvas).x, window.size(canvas).y)
    img.blit(painted, canvas, (0, 0))
  }
  canvas = window.surface(w, "canvas", [12, 24, size.x - 24, size.y - 36], 1)
  window.clear(canvas, #f4efe6)
  if (painted != null) {
    window.blit(canvas, painted, (0, 0))
  }
}

-~> Turns the splash into the app window.
function become_app() {
  window.set(w, {"frameless": false, "floating": false, "scale": 2, "size": (320, 200),
    "min_size": (200, 120), "resizable": true, "center": true, "title": "splash 🦎 app"})
  paint_app()
}

-~> A dab of ink on the canvas, and the part of it that changed.
function dab(p, pressure) {
  let r = 1.5 + pressure * 5
  let rows = []
  for (let dy = -math.ceil(r); dy <= math.ceil(r); dy += 1) {
    let half = math.sqrt(math.max(0, r * r - dy * dy))
    push(rows, [p.y + dy, p.x - half, p.x + half])
  }
  window.spans(canvas, rows, #2b3a55)
  window.dirty(canvas, [p.x - r - 1, p.y - r - 1, r * 2 + 2, r * 2 + 2])
}

-~> Every point the pen went through since the last frame, joined.
function paint_samples(samples) {
  for (let i = 0; i < len(samples); i += 1) {
    let s = samples[i]
    if (s.surface == "canvas") {
      if (last != null) {
        let steps = math.max(1, math.ceil(math.dist(last, s.surface_pos) / 2.0))
        for (let k = 1; k <= steps; k += 1) {
          dab(last + (s.surface_pos - last) * (k / float(steps)), s.pressure)
        }
      }
      last = s.surface_pos
    }
  }
}

paint_splash(0)
window.run(w, function(events) {
  for (let i = 0; i < len(events); i += 1) {
    let e = events[i]
    if (e.type == "key" && e.key == "Escape") {
      return false
    } elif (canvas == null && e.type == "down") {
      started = -100000
    } elif (canvas != null && e.type == "down" && e.surface == "canvas") {
      last = e.surface_pos
      dab(last, e.pen.pressure)
    } elif (canvas != null && e.type == "move" && last != null) {
      paint_samples(e.samples)
    } elif (e.type == "up") {
      last = null
    } elif (e.type == "resize") {
      paint_app()
    }
  }
  if (canvas == null) {
    let progress = math.min(1, (time.ms() - started) / 2000.0)
    paint_splash(progress)
    if (headless) {
      img.save(window.screenshot(w), shots[0])
      progress = 1
    }
    if (progress >= 1) {
      become_app()
    }
  } elif (headless) {
    img.save(window.screenshot(w), shots[1])
    return false
  }
})

========================================
terrarium/lib/json.liz
========================================

-~> json: reading and writing JSON.
-~>   json.parse(text)               the value of the JSON text
-~>   json.write(value, indent = 0)  the JSON text of value; with an indent
-~>                                  above 0, one item per line, nested by
-~>                                  that many spaces
-~> Objects are hashes, arrays are arrays, strings are strings, true and
-~> false are booleans and null is null. A number without a fraction or an
-~> exponent is an INTEGER (a FLOAT when it is too big for one), any other is
-~> a FLOAT. When writing, a COLOR becomes its "#rrggbb" text and a POINT
-~> becomes [x, y] (or [x, y, z]). A hash key that is not a string is
-~> written with str(). A value that can't be JSON (a function) or that
-~> contains itself is an error.
-~>
-~> The module runs in an environment of its own (see terrarium/lizard.go),
-~> and its helpers live inside the function below, so only parse and
-~> write become the json builtins.

let json = function() {
  -~> Objects and arrays nest at most this deep, so a hostile input can't
  -~> overflow the stack.
  let max_depth = 100

  -~> ---- reading ----

  -~> Stops with a message that says where in the text the problem is.
  function fail(p, message) {
    let row = 1
    let col = 1
    for (let k = 0; k < p.i && k < p.n; k += 1) {
      if (p.c[k] == "\n") {
        row += 1
        col = 1
      } else {
        col += 1
      }
    }
    error("json.parse: line " + str(row) + ", column " + str(col) + ": " + message)
  }

  -~> The text of characters a to b of the input.
  function chunk(p, a, b) {
    text.join(list.slice(p.c, a, b), "")
  }

  -~> A character for a message: control characters are shown by their code.
  function show(c) {
    if (c === null) {
      return "the end of the text"
    }
    if (c < " ") {
      return "character code " + str(text.code(c, 0))
    }
    "'" + c + "'"
  }

  function skip(p) {
    while (p.i < p.n) {
      let c = p.c[p.i]
      if (c == " " || c == "\n" || c == "\t" || c == "\r") {
        p.i += 1
      } else {
        return null
      }
    }
  }

  function is_digit(c) {
    c != null && c >= "0" && c <= "9"
  }

  -~> The word true, false or null, and the value it stands for.
  function word(p, name, value) {
    let found = chunk(p, p.i, p.i + len(name))
    if (found !== name) {
      fail(p, "expected " + name + ", found '" + found + "' (the only words are true, false and null)")
    }
    p.i += len(name)
    value
  }

  let hex_digits = "0123456789abcdef"

  -~> The four hex digits at the cursor as a number; moves past them.
  function hex4(p) {
    if (p.i + 4 > p.n) {
      fail(p, "a \\u escape needs 4 hex digits")
    }
    let n = 0
    for (let k = 0; k < 4; k += 1) {
      let d = text.find(hex_digits, text.lower(p.c[p.i + k]))
      if (d < 0) {
        p.i += k
        fail(p, "a \\u escape needs 4 hex digits, got " + show(p.c[p.i]))
      }
      n = n * 16 + d
    }
    p.i += 4
    n
  }

  let simple = {"\"": "\"", "\\": "\\", "/": "/", "b": text.char(8), "f": text.char(12), "n": "\n", "r": "\r", "t": "\t"}

  -~> The character of the escape after a backslash. The cursor is on the
  -~> letter after the backslash.
  function escape_char(p) {
    let e = p.c[p.i]
    let plain = simple[e]
    if (plain !== null) {
      p.i += 1
      return plain
    }
    if (e != "u") {
      fail(p, "bad escape \\" + e)
    }
    p.i += 1
    let code = hex4(p)
    if (code >= 56320 && code <= 57343) {
      fail(p, "a low surrogate (\\udc00 to \\udfff) must come right after a high one (\\ud800 to \\udbff)")
    }
    if (code >= 55296 && code <= 56319) {
      if (p.i + 1 < p.n && p.c[p.i] == "\\" && p.c[p.i + 1] == "u") {
        p.i += 2
        let low = hex4(p)
        if (low >= 56320 && low <= 57343) {
          return text.char(65536 + (code - 55296) * 1024 + (low - 56320))
        }
      }
      fail(p, "a high surrogate must be followed by a low one (\\udc00 to \\udfff)")
    }
    text.char(code)
  }

  -~> The string at the cursor (on its opening quote).
  function read_string(p) {
    let opening = p.i
    p.i += 1
    let parts = []
    let start = p.i
    while (true) {
      if (p.i >= p.n) {
        p.i = opening
        fail(p, "this string is never closed")
      }
      let c = p.c[p.i]
      if (c == "\"") {
        push(parts, chunk(p, start, p.i))
        p.i += 1
        return text.join(parts, "")
      }
      if (c == "\\") {
        push(parts, chunk(p, start, p.i))
        p.i += 1
        if (p.i >= p.n) {
          fail(p, "the text ends inside an escape")
        }
        push(parts, escape_char(p))
        start = p.i
      } elif (c < " ") {
        fail(p, "a string can't hold the " + show(c) + " as it is, write it as an escape")
      } else {
        p.i += 1
      }
    }
  }

  function read_number(p) {
    let start = p.i
    if (p.c[p.i] == "-") {
      p.i += 1
    }
    if (!is_digit(p.c[p.i])) {
      fail(p, "a number needs a digit here")
    }
    if (p.c[p.i] == "0") {
      p.i += 1
      if (is_digit(p.c[p.i])) {
        fail(p, "a number can't start with extra zeros")
      }
    } else {
      while (is_digit(p.c[p.i])) {
        p.i += 1
      }
    }
    if (p.c[p.i] == ".") {
      p.i += 1
      if (!is_digit(p.c[p.i])) {
        fail(p, "a number needs a digit after the point")
      }
      while (is_digit(p.c[p.i])) {
        p.i += 1
      }
    }
    if (p.c[p.i] == "e" || p.c[p.i] == "E") {
      p.i += 1
      if (p.c[p.i] == "+" || p.c[p.i] == "-") {
        p.i += 1
      }
      if (!is_digit(p.c[p.i])) {
        fail(p, "a number needs a digit in its exponent")
      }
      while (is_digit(p.c[p.i])) {
        p.i += 1
      }
    }
    let n = text.number(chunk(p, start, p.i))
    if (n === null) {
      p.i = start
      fail(p, "this number is too big")
    }
    n
  }

  function read_array(p, depth) {
    p.i += 1
    let out = []
    skip(p)
    if (p.i < p.n && p.c[p.i] == "]") {
      p.i += 1
      return out
    }
    while (true) {
      push(out, read_value(p, depth + 1))
      skip(p)
      if (p.i >= p.n) {
        fail(p, "the array is never closed, expected , or ]")
      }
      let c = p.c[p.i]
      p.i += 1
      if (c == "]") {
        return out
      }
      if (c != ",") {
        p.i -= 1
        fail(p, "expected , or ] in an array, got " + show(c))
      }
    }
  }

  function read_object(p, depth) {
    p.i += 1
    let out = {}
    skip(p)
    if (p.i < p.n && p.c[p.i] == "}") {
      p.i += 1
      return out
    }
    while (true) {
      skip(p)
      if (p.i >= p.n) {
        fail(p, "the object is never closed")
      }
      if (p.c[p.i] != "\"") {
        fail(p, "expected a string key in an object, got " + show(p.c[p.i]))
      }
      let key = read_string(p)
      skip(p)
      if (p.i >= p.n || p.c[p.i] != ":") {
        fail(p, "expected : after the key \"" + key + "\"")
      }
      p.i += 1
      out[key] = read_value(p, depth + 1)
      skip(p)
      if (p.i >= p.n) {
        fail(p, "the object is never closed, expected , or }")
      }
      let c = p.c[p.i]
      p.i += 1
      if (c == "}") {
        return out
      }
      if (c != ",") {
        p.i -= 1
        fail(p, "expected , or } in an object, got " + show(c))
      }
    }
  }

  function read_value(p, depth) {
    if (depth > max_depth) {
      fail(p, "nested more than " + str(max_depth) + " levels deep")
    }
    skip(p)
    if (p.i >= p.n) {
      fail(p, "the text ends where a value should be")
    }
    let c = p.c[p.i]
    if (c == "{") {
      return read_object(p, depth)
    }
    if (c == "[") {
      return read_array(p, depth)
    }
    if (c == "\"") {
      return read_string(p)
    }
    if (c == "t") {
      return word(p, "true", true)
    }
    if (c == "f") {
      return word(p, "false", false)
    }
    if (c == "n") {
      return word(p, "null", null)
    }
    if (c == "-" || is_digit(c)) {
      return read_number(p)
    }
    fail(p, "unexpected " + show(c) + ", expected a value")
  }

  -~> json.parse(text) is the value of the JSON text.
  function parse(source) {
    if (type(source) != "STRING") {
      error("json.parse: want a STRING text, got " + type(source))
    }
    let chars = text.split(source, "")
    let p = {"c": chars, "i": 0, "n": len(chars)}
    let v = read_value(p, 0)
    skip(p)
    if (p.i < p.n) {
      fail(p, "unexpected " + show(p.c[p.i]) + " after the end of the value")
    }
    v
  }

  -~> ---- writing ----

  -~> The escapes of the control characters, as [character, escape] pairs.
  function control_table() {
    let names = {8: "\\b", 9: "\\t", 10: "\\n", 12: "\\f", 13: "\\r"}
    let out = []
    for (let k = 0; k < 32; k += 1) {
      let e = names[k]
      if (e === null) {
        e = "\\u00" + hex_digits[k // 16] + hex_digits[k % 16]
      }
      push(out, [text.char(k), e])
    }
    out
  }

  let controls = control_table()

  -~> The JSON text of a string, with its quotes. It makes a few passes over
  -~> the whole text, not one call for each character, so a long text is
  -~> cheap.
  function quote(s) {
    if (text.find(s, "\\") >= 0) {
      s = text.replace(s, "\\", "\\\\")
    }
    if (text.find(s, "\"") >= 0) {
      s = text.replace(s, "\"", "\\\"")
    }
    for (let k = 0; k < 32; k += 1) {
      if (text.find(s, controls[k][0]) >= 0) {
        s = text.replace(s, controls[k][0], controls[k][1])
      }
    }
    "\"" + s + "\""
  }

  -~> The items of an array or object between open and close: on one line
  -~> when indent is 0, else one per line, each indent spaces further in than
  -~> pad.
  function enclose(open, items, close, indent, pad) {
    if (len(items) == 0) {
      return open + close
    }
    if (indent == 0) {
      return open + text.join(items, ",") + close
    }
    let inner = pad + " " * indent
    open + "\n" + inner + text.join(items, ",\n" + inner) + "\n" + pad + close
  }

  function write_value(v, indent, depth, pad) {
    if (depth > max_depth) {
      error("json.write: the value is nested more than " + str(max_depth) + " levels deep (does it contain itself?)")
    }
    let t = type(v)
    if (t == "STRING") {
      return quote(v)
    }
    if (t == "INTEGER" || t == "FLOAT") {
      return str(v)
    }
    if (t == "BOOLEAN") {
      if (v) {
        return "true"
      }
      return "false"
    }
    if (t == "NULL") {
      return "null"
    }
    if (t == "COLOR") {
      return quote(str(v))
    }
    if (t == "POINT") {
      let coords = [str(v.x), str(v.y)]
      -~> Reading z of a point with two coordinates is an error.
      try {
        push(coords, str(v.z))
      } catch {
      }
      return "[" + text.join(coords, ",") + "]"
    }
    let inner = pad + " " * indent
    if (t == "ARRAY") {
      let items = []
      for (let k = 0; k < len(v); k += 1) {
        push(items, write_value(v[k], indent, depth + 1, inner))
      }
      return enclose("[", items, "]", indent, pad)
    }
    if (t == "HASH") {
      let colon = ":"
      if (indent > 0) {
        colon = ": "
      }
      let items = []
      let names = keys(v)
      for (let k = 0; k < len(names); k += 1) {
        let name = names[k]
        if (type(name) != "STRING") {
          name = str(name)
        }
        push(items, quote(name) + colon + write_value(v[names[k]], indent, depth + 1, inner))
      }
      return enclose("{", items, "}", indent, pad)
    }
    error("json.write: can't write a " + t + " as JSON")
  }

  -~> json.write(value, indent = 0) is the JSON text of value.
  function write(value, indent = 0) {
    if (type(indent) != "INTEGER" || indent < 0 || indent > 16) {
      error("json.write: the indent must be an INTEGER from 0 to 16, got " + str(indent))
    }
    write_value(value, indent, 0, "")
  }

  {"parse": parse, "write": write}
}()
